- **show-dont-tell**: Visualise complexity and big ideas. Use when an answer involves many connected parts (services, modules, call chains, dependencies), a multi-step or multi-actor flow, a cycle or fan-out, a state machine, or several options weighed on several criteria; a big idea or design to explain in stages; also when the user asks for a diagram, walkthrough, demo or picture. Judge whether it is complex enough, offer picture options, draw the one picked. Skip for simple answers. ([danielriddell21/skills](https://github.com/danielriddell21/skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/picture-this/skills/show-dont-tell) · ★1)
- **show-dont-tell**: Turn plans, research, concepts and finished work into short videos people actually watch, instead of walls of text. Use when the user asks for a video, an animation, motion graphics, an explainer, a narrated walkthrough, a feature demo or PR video with a screen recording, a launch film, a UGC-style clip, a voiceover, or wants to present an idea, plan or update visually. Covers choosing the kind of video, the tools (Remotion, HyperFrames, Manim, Playwright recording, Three.js), voice, music, images, and a storyboard-to-critique creative process. ([AnsarUllahAnasZ360/show-dont-tell](https://github.com/AnsarUllahAnasZ360/show-dont-tell/tree/HEAD/skills/show-dont-tell))
- **show-dont-tell**: Use when explaining something with structure a reader has to hold in their head, such as a chain of calls or components waiting on each other, requests over time, state transitions, or a data model, in an issue, PR description, code review finding, review reply, or chat message; whenever a change alters what a user sees on screen; and whenever you report work that produced something visual, such as screenshots, Storybook stories, UI states, or rendered diagrams. ([danielbachhuber/skills](https://github.com/danielbachhuber/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/show-dont-tell))

- **show-dont-tell**: Show, don't tell — plans, specs, and approvals ship as mermaid diagrams, never walls of text, because a picture is worth a thousand words.
